\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}&\ldots\\
\hline\cr\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots\end{array}\right]\,.

In principle, one could initially consider a pointer with a Hilbert space dimension larger than required for the desired
\lambda
. However, the energy levels exceeding
\lambda d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
would have to be truncated before the preparation step to avoid unnecessary additional energy costs. The general form of the unitaries realising such unbiased measurement procedures is summarised below.

Lemma 2

Let
\mathcal{M}_{U}(\beta)
be an unbiased finite-resource (
\Delta E<\infty,T=1/\beta>0
) measurement procedure with
unitary
correlating step
\mathcal{E}_{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$\mathrm{I\hskip-0.5ptI}$}}}
using a pointer system Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}
and Hamiltonian
H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}
with
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}=\lambda d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
for
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}
. The unitary map
U
realizing the correlating step, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}_{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$\mathrm{I\hskip-0.5ptI}$}}}[\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}]

\displaystyle=\,U\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}U^{\dagger},

(A.53)

can be split into two consecutive unitary operations,
U=V\tilde{U}
, where
\tilde{U}
and
V
are of the form

\displaystyle\tilde{U}

\displaystyle=\,\sum\limits_{i=0}^{d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}-1}\left|\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right|_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes\tilde{U}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(i)$}}},

(A.54)

\displaystyle V

\displaystyle=\,\sum\limits_{i,j=0}^{d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}-1}\sum\limits_{m=1}^{\lambda}\left|\right.\!{j}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right|_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes\left|\right.\!{\tilde{\psi}_{m}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(i)$}}}}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{\tilde{\psi}_{m}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(j)$}}}}\!\left.\right|_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}},

(A.55)

and
\tilde{U}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(i)$}}}
are arbitrary unitaries on
\mathcal{H}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}
.

Proof of Lemma

A.V
. The structure of the unitaries in the correlating step can be understood by noting that unitaries have only a single non-trivial Kraus operator. The operators
\tilde{U}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(i)$}}}
in the first unitary
\tilde{U}
then simply correspond to the single Kraus operators of the maps
\mathcal{E}_{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$\mathrm{I\hskip-0.5ptI}$}}}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(i)$}}}
from Eq. (
A.IV.3
), rearranging the joint density matrix only in the subspaces of fixed
\left|\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right\rangle_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
, creating the distinction between the sub-blocks
\tilde{A}_{ji}
for different
j
. The second part, realized by the unitary
V
then just swaps these sub-blocks, such that all
\tilde{A}_{0i}
are left in the subspace corresponding to
\left|\right.\!{0}\!\left.\right\rangle_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
and
\Pi_{i}
, all
\tilde{A}_{1i}
are left in the subspace corresponding to
\left|\right.\!{1}\!\left.\right\rangle_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
and
\Pi_{i}
, and so forth. The only freedom in choosing unitary correlation steps for unbiased measurements hence lies in the choice of the
\tilde{U}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(i)$}}}
. ∎

A.VI
Unbiased and Non-invasive Measurements are Faithful

With the compact result of Lemmas
A.IV.3
and
A.V
at hand, let us now briefly return to the relationship between the properties unbiasedness and non-invasiveness. As we have now seen, measurement procedures that are unbiased for all initial system states
\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
are required to map the subspace of the joint system-pointer Hilbert space corresponding to the image of the operator
\left|\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right|_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}
to the subspace corresponding to the image of
\mathds{1}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes\Pi_{i}
for all
i
. At this point it becomes clear that, formulating the analogous statements to Lemmas
A.IV.3
and
A.V
for measurement procedures that are non-invasive instead of unbiased for all
\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
, results in maps from the subspace corresponding to the image of the operator
\left|\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right|_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}
to itself. Since the image of
\left|\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right|_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}
is spanned by the set of non-trivial joint eigenvectors of the set of projectors
\{\left|\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right|_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes\Pi_{j}\}_{j}
, and the image of
\mathds{1}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes\Pi_{i}
is spanned by the set of non-trivial joint eigenvectors of the set of projectors
\{\left|\right.\!{j}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{j}\!\left.\right|_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes\Pi_{i}\}_{j}
, a map that is supposed to satisfy both unbiasedness and non-invasiveness for all
\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
must be a map from the image of
\left|\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right|_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}
to the span of the set of non-trivial eigenvectors of
\left|\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right|_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes\Pi_{i}
, such that
\mathrm{tr}(\left|\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right|_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes\Pi_{i}\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})=\rho_{ii}\ \forall i
. By construction, one thus has
\sum_{i}\mathrm{tr}(\left|\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right|_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes\Pi_{i}\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})=\sum_{i}\rho_{ii}=1
, and the measurement is faithful.

A.VII
Maximally Correlating Unbiased Measurements

To gain further insight into the fundamental limitations of non-ideal measurements, we now wish to focus on a special case where Lemma
A.V
applies, that is, an unbiased measurement procedure with unitary correlating step, such that — at least for the purpose of controlling their interaction — the joint system of pointer and measured system can be considered closed. That is, procedures where
\mathcal{E}_{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$\mathrm{I\hskip-0.5ptI}$}}}[\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}]=U\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}U^{\dagger}
. Apart from this restriction, we will only consider preparation steps that modify the temperature of the initial pointer system, i.e.
\mathcal{E}_{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$\mathrm{I}$}}}
is a refrigeration step.
In such a scenario,
T=1/\beta
might be the initial temperature of the pointer, or, e.g., one below the ambient temperature, reached by investing energy for cooling the pointer. For such measurement procedures, we now wish to find the maximum attainable correlation between the system and pointer. As we show, there is an algebraic maximum
C_{\text{max}}
for the correlations that can be unitarily created between the system and the thermal pointer, regardless of the initial system state
\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
. Recall the definition of the correlation function in Eq. (
A.19
), which we rewrite as

\displaystyle C(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})

\displaystyle:=\,\sum\limits_{i}\mathrm{tr}\bigl[\tilde{\Pi}_{ii}\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}\bigr]\,,

(A.56)

by making the association
\tilde{\Pi}_{ij}=\left|\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right|\otimes\Pi_{j}\;\forall\,i,j
. By definition, the correlation function is only sensitive to terms appearing along the diagonal w.r.t. any chosen common eigenbasis (with nontrivial eigenvalues) of the set of operators
\tilde{\Pi}_{ii}
. We refer to the subspace of
\mathcal{H}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}
spanned by these eigenvectors as
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{corr}}
, and to its complement as
\mathrm{H}_{\mathrm{nc}}
, such that
\mathcal{H}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes\mathcal{H}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}=\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{corr}}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{nc}}
. In particular, this implies that the unitary transformation achieving the algebraic maximum (over all unitaries
U_{\mathrm{unb}}
realizing unbiased measurement procedures in the sense of Lemma
A.V
)

\displaystyle\max_{U_{\mathrm{unb}}}C(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})=C_{\text{max}}

(A.57)

is not unique since
C(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})
is invariant under operations of the form
U_{\mathrm{corr}}\oplus U_{\mathrm{nc}}
, where
U_{\mathrm{corr}}
and
U_{\mathrm{nc}}
act nontrivially only on the subspaces
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{corr}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{nc}}
, respectively.

Within the orbit of all unitaries that one may perform (including those corresponding to biased measurements) on
\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes\tau_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}
, the global maximum value of the function
C(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})
is achieved when the state
\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}
is block-diagonal w.r.t. to the subspace partition into
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{corr}}\oplus\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{nc}}
, and the eigenvalues of the joint final state restricted to the
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}
-dimensional correlated subspace
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{corr}}
, given by
\tilde{\rho}_{\mathrm{corr}}=\Pi_{\mathrm{corr}}\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}\Pi_{\mathrm{corr}}
with
\Pi_{\mathrm{corr}}=\sum_{i}\left|\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right|\otimes\Pi_{i}
, are the
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}
largest eigenvalues of
\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}
. These eigenvalues depend on
\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
. However, we have to take into account unbiasedness and the fact that we are looking for a unitary. In particular, from Eq. (
A.48
) we know that the
\Pi_{i}
must all have the same rank, namely
\operatorname{rank}(\Pi_{i})=d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}=2^{N-1}\ \forall\,i
. It then becomes apparent that one is restricted to selecting the
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
largest eigenvalues of
\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}=\tau_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}(\beta)
for each of the
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
subspaces corresponding to the image of a projector
\left|\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right|\otimes\Pi_{i}
. Since the assignment of eigenvalues to each subspace labelled by
i
is the same, and the corresponding matrix elements of the initial system state sum to 1, the maximal achievable correlation is independent of
\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
.
In the notation of Eq. (
A.1
), this corresponds to all probability populations that belong to the sector where
(k=0)
, i.e.,

\displaystyle C_{\text{max}}(\beta)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}-1}\sum_{j=0}^{d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}-1}\rho_{ii}\,p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{j}\,=\,\tfrac{1}{\mathcal{Z}}\sum_{i=0}^{d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}-1}e^{-\beta E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}}\;.

(A.58)

As mentioned in Eq. (
A.45
), this function is independent of the system. The remaining probability weights (i.e., the
p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}_{j}
for
k\neq 0
and
j=0,\ldots,d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}-1
) are distributed in the non-correlated subspace.

The corresponding general form of the joint final state
\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}
of any unitarily maximally correlating unbiased measurement procedure (starting from an initially thermal pointer state) can then be compactly specified in terms of its correlation matrix
\Gamma_{U_{C_{\mathrm{max}}}}
as defined in Eq. (
A.IV.3
). To write
\Gamma_{U_{C_{\mathrm{max}}}}
in a simple form, let
a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}
for
i=0,1,\ldots,d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
be
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\times d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
Hermitian matrices whose eigenvalues are the
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
largest eigenvalues of the initial pointer state
\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}=\tau_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}(\beta)
, i.e.,

\displaystyle a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}=M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}\bigl(\operatorname{diag}(p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{0},p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{1},\ldots,p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}-1}\bigr){M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}}^{\dagger},

(A.59)

where the
M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}
are
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\times d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
unitary matrices. The correlation matrix
\Gamma_{U_{C_{\mathrm{max}}}}
is then of the form

\displaystyle\Gamma_{U_{C_{\mathrm{max}}}}

}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.56064pt}{9.95863pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\raisebox{2.0pt}{\footnotesize{$\rho_{22}a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{2}$}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \immediate;
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}&\ldots\\
\hline\cr\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots\end{array}\right]\,,

where the block matrices on the diagonal (blue) are the corresponding entries of
\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}
restricted to the correlated subspace
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{corr}}
, whereas the block matrices on the off-diagonal of
\Gamma_{U_{C_{\mathrm{max}}}}
(here shown in green) correspond to the diagonal blocks of
\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}
restricted to the non-correlated subspace
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{nc}}
. Additional off-diagonal entries may appear in the projection of
\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}
onto
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{nc}}
between blocks
\tilde{A}_{ij}
and
\tilde{A}_{i^{\prime}j}
with the same column index
j
but different row indices
i\neq i^{\prime}
with
i,i^{\prime}\neq j
, while maintaining an unbiased measurement with maximal correlation. The additional constraint of Eq. (
A.31
) ensuring an unbiased measurement procedure can here be written as

\mathrm{tr}[a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{j}]+\sum_{i\,,\\
i\neq j}\mathrm{tr}[\tilde{A}_{ij}]=1,\,\forall\,j
. The remaining freedom of applying unitaries that leave the subspaces
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{corr}}
and
\mathcal{H}_{\mathrm{nc}}
invariant and are compatible with unbiasedness can be used for minimization of the corresponding energy cost. Before we discuss this procedure for arbitrary system and pointer dimensions, it will be instructive to consider the special case where the system is a single qubit and the pointer consists of
N
identical two-level systems.

A.VIII
Optimally Correlating Unitary for a Single-Qubit System and
N
-Qubit Pointer

In the previous appendix, we have identified the structure of all unitarily correlating unbiased measurements that create maximal correlations
C_{\mathrm{max}}
(a subclass of the maps
\mathcal{E}_{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$\mathrm{I\hskip-0.5ptI}$}}}
). We are now interested in further restricting this set of measurements to identify those unitaries that achieve
C_{\mathrm{max}}
for the least energy.
That is, we wish to determine the optimal
U_{\mathrm{opt}}
which solves the optimisation problem

\displaystyle\min_{U_{\text{corr}}}\,\Delta E_{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$\mathrm{I\hskip-0.5ptI}$}}}\;\;\text{s.t.}\;\;C(\tilde{\rho}_{{}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}})

\displaystyle=C_{\text{max}}\,.

(A.64)

For arbitrary system dimensions and Hamiltonians, the explicit form of the solutions
U_{\mathrm{opt}}
is rather involved and the proofs of optimality become very technical in nature. Before we move on to such general cases in Appendix
A.IX
, let us therefore here illustrate the general method by focusing on an example of interest.

Here, we consider a two-dimensional system, i.e., a qubit with Hilbert space
\mathcal{H}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}=\mathbb{C}^{2}
, dimensions
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}=2
, and a Hamiltonian
H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
with eigenstates
\left|\right.\!{0}\!\left.\right\rangle_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
and
\left|\right.\!{1}\!\left.\right\rangle_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
and spectral decomposition
H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}=E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\left|\right.\!{1}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{1}\!\left.\right|_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
. In addition, we assume that the system state is initially unknown such that the corresponding density operator is maximally mixed,
\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathds{1}_{2}
. Meanwhile, we consider a measurement apparatus modelled as an
N
-qubit pointer,
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}=2^{N}
, where each qubit has the same local Hamiltonian with vanishing ground state energy and energy gap matching the system energy gap,
E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}=E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
. The total pointer Hamiltonian is thus
H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}=\sum_{k=0}^{1}\sum_{i=0}^{2^{N-1}-1}E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}_{i}\left|\right.\!{E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}_{i}}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}_{i}}\!\left.\right|
, where we have adopted the sector notation introduced in Eq. (
A.1
). Note that the pointer spectrum is highly degenerate since there are
2^{N}
eigenvalues but only
N+1
different energy levels,
E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}_{i}/E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\in\{0,1,\ldots,N\}
. With respect to the energy eigenbasis the initial pointer state before the correlating step is

\displaystyle\tau_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}(\beta)^{\otimes N}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{1}\sum_{i=0}^{2^{N-1}-1}p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}_{i}\left|\right.\!{E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}_{i}}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}_{i}}\!\left.\right|,

(A.65)

with
p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}_{i}=e^{-\beta E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}_{i}}/\mathcal{Z}
and
\mathcal{Z}=\mathrm{tr}(e^{-\beta H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}})=\sum_{i,k}e^{-\beta E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}_{i}}
. For this setting, we will now solve the optimization problem of Eq. (
A.64
) for
\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}=U_{\mathrm{corr}}\,(\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes\tau_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}(\beta)^{\otimes N}\,)U_{\mathrm{corr}}^{\dagger}
.

From (
A.58
), the maximum algebraic correlation achievable between an
N-
qubit pointer and a qubit system is

\displaystyle C_{\text{max}}(\beta)

\displaystyle=\;\tfrac{1}{\mathcal{Z}}\sum_{i=0}^{2^{N-1}-1}e^{-\beta E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}}\,,

(A.66)

and the post-interaction correlation matrix associated with this scenario is given by

\displaystyle\Gamma_{U_{C_{\text{max}}}}=\,\left[\begin{array}[]{c|c}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 3.54 -3.94 M 3.54 1.6 L 3.54 8.24 C 3.54 11.3 1.07 13.78 -1.99 13.78 L 1.99 13.78 C -1.07 13.78 -3.54 11.3 -3.54 8.24 L -3.54 1.6 C -3.54 -1.46 -1.07 -3.94 1.99 -3.94 L -1.99 -3.94 C 1.07 -3.94 3.54 -1.46 3.54 1.6 Z M -3.54 13.78}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.56064pt}{9.95863pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\raisebox{2.0pt}{\footnotesize{$\rho_{00}a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{0}$}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \immediate;

Here,
a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{0}
and
a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{1}
are
2^{N-1}\times 2^{N-1}
dimensional Hermitian matrices whose eigenvalues are the
2^{N-1}
largest eigenvalues of
\tau_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}^{\otimes N}
, that is, there are a unitary matrices
M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{0}
and
M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{1}
such that

\displaystyle a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}=M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}\bigl(\text{diag}(p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{0},\cdots,p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{2^{N-1}-1})\bigl){M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}}^{\dagger}\ \ \text{for}\ i=0,1.

(A.69)

In our example, we further have
\rho_{00}=\rho_{11}=\tfrac{1}{2}
but we leave the symbols
\rho_{00}
and
\rho_{11}
for clarity where necessary. For the interaction to be unbiased according to Eq. (
A.VII
), there is now no choice but to set
\tilde{A}_{01}=a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{1}
and
\tilde{A}_{10}=a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{0}
, where

\displaystyle a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{i}=M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{i}\bigl(\operatorname{diag}(p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{0},p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{1},\ldots,p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{2^{N-1}-1}\bigr){M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{i}}^{\dagger},

(A.70)

and
M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{i}
for
i=0,1
are unitaries not yet fixed by the requirements of unbiasedness or maximal correlation. The eigenvalues
\{p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{0},p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{1},\ldots,p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{2^{N-1}-1}\}
of
a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{0}
and
a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{1}
correspond to the second (smaller) half of the eigenvalues of
\tau_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}^{\otimes N}
, and hence we have
\mathrm{tr}(a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i})+\mathrm{tr}(a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{i})=1\ \forall\,i
. The correlation matrix becomes

\displaystyle\Gamma_{U_{C_{\text{max}}}}=\,\left[\begin{array}[]{c|c}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}&\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}
{}{{}}{}{}{}{}{{}}{}{{}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{{}{}{{}}}{{}{}{{}}}{}{}{}\lxSVG@fillstroke\lxSVG@drawpath@unclipped{M 0 0 M 3.54 -3.94 M 3.54 1.6 L 3.54 8.24 C 3.54 11.3 1.07 13.78 -1.99 13.78 L 1.99 13.78 C -1.07 13.78 -3.54 11.3 -3.54 8.24 L -3.54 1.6 C -3.54 -1.46 -1.07 -3.94 1.99 -3.94 L -1.99 -3.94 C 1.07 -3.94 3.54 -1.46 3.54 1.6 Z M -3.54 13.78}{} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{}{}{}{}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-2.56064pt}{9.95863pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform} \pgfsys@hbox{60}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\raisebox{2.0pt}{\footnotesize{$\rho_{11}a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{1}$}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \immediate;
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\end{array}\right]\,.

In the present case where
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}=2
and a maximally correlated, unbiased measurement, the correlation matrix
\Gamma_{U_{C_{\text{max}}}}
indeed catches all nonzero elements of
\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}
, which is block diagonal,

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}

\displaystyle=\diag(\rho_{00}a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{0},\rho_{11}a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{1},\rho_{00}a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{0},\rho_{11}a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{1}).

(A.73)

In general, for
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}>2
, additional nonzero off-diagonal elements may appear in
\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}
that are not explicitly captured by
\Gamma_{U_{C_{\text{max}}}}
.

The cost of correlating, and the function we wish to minimise, is given by the energy difference of the initial and final states,

\displaystyle\Delta E_{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$\mathrm{I\hskip-0.5ptI}$}}}=E(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})-E({\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}).

(A.74)

Since the initial energy is fixed by the initial temperature (and any preparation one wishes to include), we focus on minimising
E(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})
.
In order to facilitate the computation, it will be useful to decompose
E(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})
in terms of the correlated and non-correlated subspaces

\displaystyle E(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})

\displaystyle=E_{\mathrm{corr}}(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})+E_{\mathrm{nc}}(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})

(A.75)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}[\sum_{i}\tilde{\Pi}_{ii}H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}]\,+\,\mathrm{tr}[\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}i,j\\
i\neq j\end{subarray}}\tilde{\Pi}_{ij}H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}\;\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}],

where, again we have
\tilde{\Pi}_{ij}=\left|\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right|\otimes\Pi_{j}
and the combined system-pointer Hamiltonian is
H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}=H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes\mathds{1}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}+\mathds{1}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\otimes H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}
.
For our qubit example, measured by an
N
-qubit pointer, this becomes

\displaystyle E(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})

\displaystyle=\mathrm{tr}[(\tilde{\Pi}_{00}+\tilde{\Pi}_{11})H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}]

(A.76)

\displaystyle\ \ +\mathrm{tr}[(\tilde{\Pi}_{01}+\tilde{\Pi}_{10})H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}\;\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}].

The class of unitaries that achieve
C_{\text{max}}
rearranges the elements of
\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}
to place the ‘heaviest’ populations of probabilities (eigenvalues of
\tau_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}
) into the correlated subspaces of
\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}
. From this constraint we already know which elements (eigenvalues) of the post-interaction state
\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}
are assigned to which subspaces. In order to minimise the energy, one must therefore find the optimal assignment of the energy eigenbasis to these subspaces, which amounts to determining the
\Pi_{i}
.

We now proceed as follows: First, we will minimise the energy in the correlated subspaces, after which we will minimise the energy in the non-correlated subspaces, a strategy that presents a global energy minimum for the entire state.

Noting that
\tilde{\Pi}_{ij}\perp\tilde{\Pi}_{i^{\prime}j^{\prime}}
whenever
i\neq i^{\prime}
or
j\neq j^{\prime}
we observe that also
(\tilde{\Pi}_{00}+\tilde{\Pi}_{11})
is a projector. We can hence rewrite the first term on right-hand side of Eq. (
A.76
) as

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\bigl[\sum\limits_{i=0,1}\tilde{\Pi}_{ii}\,H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}\,\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}\bigr]\,=\,\mathrm{tr}\bigl[\bigl(\sum\limits_{i=0,1}\tilde{\Pi}_{ii}\bigr)^{2}\,H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}\,\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}\bigr]

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ =\,\mathrm{tr}\bigl[\bigl(\sum\limits_{i=0,1}\tilde{\Pi}_{ii}\bigr)\,H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}\,\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}\,\bigl(\sum\limits_{j=0,1}\tilde{\Pi}_{jj}\bigr)\bigr]

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ =\,\sum\limits_{i=0,1}\mathrm{tr}\bigl[\tilde{\Pi}_{ii}\,H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}\,\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}\,\tilde{\Pi}_{ii}\bigr],

(A.77)

where we have used the orthogonality of the projectors again in the last step. Once the trace has been restricted to the subspace corresponding to the space spanned by the nontrivial eigenvectors of
\tilde{\Pi}_{ii}
, we can further rewrite Eq. (
A.77
) as

\displaystyle\sum\limits_{i=0,1}\mathrm{tr}\bigl[\tilde{\Pi}_{ii}\,H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}\,\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}\,\tilde{\Pi}_{ii}\bigr]

(A.78)

\displaystyle\ \ =\,\sum\limits_{i=0,1}\mathrm{tr}\bigl[\tilde{\Pi}_{ii}\,(H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}+H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}})\,\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}\,\tilde{\Pi}_{ii}\bigr]

\displaystyle\ \ =\,\rho_{11}\,E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\,\mathrm{tr}\bigl[a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{1}\bigr]\,+\,\sum\limits_{i=0,1}\rho_{ii}\,\mathrm{tr}\bigl[\Pi_{i}H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}\Pi_{i}\,a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}\bigr].

Here, one should note that, strictly speaking,
\Pi_{i}H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}\Pi_{i}
are
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}\times d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}
matrices, while
a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}
are
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\times d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
matrices. However, the image of
\Pi_{i}H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}\Pi_{i}
is also of dimension
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
, and one may hence think of
\Pi_{i}H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}\Pi_{i}
as nonzero
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\times d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
matrices padded by rows and columns of zeros. In a slight abuse of notation we use the same symbol for the entire operator and its nontrivial block, since it is clear from the context, which object is referred to. In particular,
(\Pi_{i}H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}\Pi_{i})\,a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}
refers to the product of two
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\times d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
matrices. To simplify the last line of Eq. (
A.78
), let us first write
a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}:=\text{diag}(p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{0},\cdots,p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{2^{N-1}-1})
, where we
may assume w.l.o.g. that
a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}
is diagonal w.r.t. the same basis as
\Pi_{i}H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}\Pi_{i}
and the populations are ordered in non-increasing order. Any mismatch can be absorbed into the choice of the
{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}}
. Further note for the first term that

\mathrm{tr}\bigl[a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{1}\bigr]=\mathrm{tr}\bigl[a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}\bigr]
, whereas the second term can be expressed as

\displaystyle\mathrm{tr}\bigl[\Pi_{i}H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}\Pi_{i}\,a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\,\mathrm{tr}\bigl[\Pi_{i}H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}\Pi_{i}\,{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}}a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}}^{\dagger}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\,\mathrm{tr}\bigl[{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}}^{\dagger}\Pi_{i}H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}\Pi_{i}\,{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}}a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}\bigr].

(A.79)

The quantity that we wish to minimize in this first step is thus of the form

\displaystyle E_{\mathrm{corr}}(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})

\displaystyle=\,\mathrm{tr}\bigl[\,\bigl(\rho_{00}{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{0}}^{\dagger}\Pi_{0}H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}\Pi_{0}{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{0}}

(A.80)

\displaystyle\ +\rho_{11}{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{1}}^{\dagger}\Pi_{1}(H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}+E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}})\Pi_{1}{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{1}}\bigr)a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}\bigr].

The minimization is to be carried out over all choices of projectors
\Pi_{i}
, i.e., choosing the basis
\{\left|\right.\!{\tilde{\psi}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(i)$}}}_{n}}\!\left.\right\rangle\}_{i,n}
in relation to the eigenbasis of
H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}
, as well as over all choices of the
{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}}
, or, in general the
{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(j)$}}}_{i}}
. While the minimisation over the
\Pi_{i}
requires some more in-depth analysis (that we perform below), a simple observation can made right away. For a given initial state, the state in the unitary orbit of the initial state with minimal energy w.r.t. to a given Hamiltonian must be diagonal in the eigenbasis of this Hamiltonian, i.e., the corresponding passive state. Meanwhile, the conditions of unbiasedness and maximal algebraic correlations impose a certain block structure once a basis has been fixed and allow for but do not require off-diagonal elements. Therefore, it is clear that the unbiased, minimal energy solution with maximal algebraic correlations must be diagonal w.r.t.
H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}
, restricting the unitaries connecting the bases
\{\left|\right.\!{\tilde{\psi}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(i)$}}}_{n}}\!\left.\right\rangle\}_{i,n}
with the eigenbasis of
H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}
as well as the unitaries
{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(j)$}}}_{i}}
to be
permutation
matrices. Moreover, suppose that for the fixed choice of
{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}}=\mathds{1}\ \forall i
one has found an optimal choice of
\Pi_{i}
. Then any nontrivial modification of any of the
{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}}
can only increase the energy in the respective subspace. Without loss of generality we therefore set
{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}}=\mathds{1}\ \forall i
.

With this, we can now rewrite the energy in the correlated subspace as

\displaystyle E_{\mathrm{corr}}(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})

\displaystyle=\,\mathrm{tr}\bigl[\,\bigl(\rho_{00}\Pi_{0}H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}\Pi_{0}

(A.81)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ +\rho_{11}\Pi_{1}(H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}+E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}})\Pi_{1}\bigr)a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}\bigr]=\mathbf{x}\cdot\mathbf{a}^{0},

where
\mathbf{a}^{0}
is the vector of diagonal entries of the matrix
a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}

and

\mathbf{x}\in\mathbb{R}^{2^{N-1}}
is the vector of diagonal entries of the matrix
\bigl(\rho_{00}\Pi_{0}H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}\Pi_{0}+\rho_{11}\Pi_{1}(H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}+E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}})\Pi_{1}\bigr)
.
Every component
x_{i}
of
\mathbf{x}
is seen to be sum of two energies from the
\Pi_{0}
and
\Pi_{1}
subspaces of
H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}
, respectively modulated by the respective system populations
\rho_{ii}
.

We now switch to a slightly less cumbersome notation for the pointer. Let the set
\mathcal{S}_{N}
with elements
s_{i}
for
i\in\{0,\cdots,d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}-1\}
be the set of energies (in units of
E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
) in the energy spectrum of the pointer, ordered in non-decreasing order, such that
H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}=\sum_{i}s_{i}\left|\right.\!{s_{i}}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{s_{i}}\!\left.\right|
and
s_{i}\leq s_{j}\;\forall i<j
. For example, a
3
-qubit pointer with gap
E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}=E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
and vanishing ground state would be associated with the set
\mathcal{S}_{3}=\{0,1,1,1,2,2,2,3\}
.
The elements of the vector
\mathbf{x}
can now be written as

\displaystyle\begin{split}x_{i}=\rho_{00}s_{j}+\rho_{11}(s_{l}+E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}})\quad&j\neq l,\\
&0\leq j,l\leq(2^{N}-1),\\
&s_{j,l}\in\mathcal{S}_{N}\;,\end{split}

(A.82)

such that the indices
j,l
are used only once. Thus, the
x_{i}
are composed by selecting pairs of elements, without replacement, from the set
\mathcal{S}_{N}
.
There are several statements we can make immediately about the set
\mathcal{S}_{N}
. First, it has
2^{N}
elements which are distributed binomially such that the energy
kE_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}
appears
{{N}\choose{k}}
times. Second, in the case that we are probing an unknown state,
\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}=\tfrac{1}{2}\mathds{1}_{2}
, the sum of the elements

Figure A.1:
The energies of a
3-
qubit pointer
\mathcal{S}_{3}=\{s_{0},\cdots,s_{7}\}
with gap
E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}=1
are arranged in non-increasing order. The schematic show two ways of choosing
x_{i}
from the set
\mathcal{S}_{3}
. The right hand side selects nearest neighbour pairs and thus represents the optimal pairing that minimises
E_{\mathrm{corr}}(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})
in (
A.84
).

of
\mathbf{x}
is constant. Namely

\displaystyle\sum_{i=0}^{2^{N-1}-1}x_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i=0}^{2^{N}-1}(s_{i}+\tfrac{1}{4}E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}})=c\,.

(A.83)

Since
x_{i}\geq 0\;\forall i
, this means we can treat the set
\left\{{x_{i}}/{c}\right\}_{i}
as a normalised probability distribution.
Let
X
denote the set of all possible vectors
\mathbf{x}
, then, minimising the energy in the correlated subspace amounts to

\displaystyle\min E_{\mathrm{corr}}(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})

\displaystyle=\min_{\mathbf{x}\in X}(\mathbf{x}\cdot\mathbf{a}^{0})\,=\,\mathbf{x}^{*}\cdot\mathbf{a}^{0}.

(A.84)

The set
X
can be understood as the set of all possible ways of choosing pairs from
\mathcal{S}_{N}
without replacement. The size (i.e., the cardinality) of
X
, denoted
|X|
, grows factorially with
N
, so searching by brute-force for the optimal vector is not feasible.
The solution
\mathbf{x}^{*}
for the minimization problem in Eq. (
A.84
) is given by the vector that pairs the smallest weights
p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}
with the largest values
x_{i}
. Specifically, let
\mathbf{v},\mathbf{w}\in\mathbb{R}^{2^{N-1}}
be two normalised vectors with their components ordered in non-increasing order such that
v_{0}\geq v_{1}\cdots
and
w_{0}\geq w_{1}\cdots
. We say that
\mathbf{v}
majorises
\mathbf{w}
, written
\mathbf{v}\succ\mathbf{w}
, when
\sum_{i}^{k}v_{i}\geq\sum_{i}^{k}w_{i}\;\forall\;k
. In other words the cumulative sum of the components of the vector
\mathbf{v}
grows faster than for
\mathbf{w}
. The vector
\mathbf{x}^{*}
that presents the solution to the optimization problem is hence the vector that majorises all other vectors
\mathbf{x}\in X
, i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathbf{x}^{*}\succ\mathbf{x}\ \qquad\forall\,\mathbf{x}\in X.

(A.85)

This vector is constructed by maximising each
x_{i}^{*}
term by term from the bottom up, populating the components of
\mathbf{x}^{*}
such that
x_{2^{N-1}-1}\geq\cdots\geq x_{0}
. This construction amounts to picking nearest neighbour pairs from the set
\mathcal{S}_{N}
, starting with the largest pair, as illustrated in Fig.
A.1
. Thus, the components of the optimal solution take the form

\displaystyle x_{i}^{*}=\rho_{00}s_{2i}+\rho_{11}(s_{2i+1}+E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}})\qquad s_{i}\in\mathcal{S}_{N}\,.

(A.86)

By construction, the majorisation of Eq. (
A.85
) is satisfied, and we have found the minimum energy solution in the correlated subspace.

By constructing
\mathbf{x}^{*}
from nearest neighbour pairs in
\mathcal{S}_{N}
, we have fixed the energy eigenbasis in the pointer Hilbert space. The projectors on the pointer are then

\displaystyle\Pi_{0}=\sum_{i=0}^{2^{N-1}-1}\left|\right.\!{s_{2i}}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{s_{2i}}\!\left.\right|,\ \ \Pi_{1}=\sum_{i=0}^{2^{N-1}-1}\left|\right.\!{s_{2i+1}}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{s_{2i+1}}\!\left.\right|\,.

(A.87)

We now proceed to minimise the energy in the non-correlated subspaces.
Following a similar calculation and series of arguments as leading to Eq. (
A.81
), we write the energy as

\displaystyle E_{\mathrm{nc}}(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})

\displaystyle=\,\mathrm{tr}\bigl[\,\rho_{11}\Pi_{1}H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}\Pi_{1}\,{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{1}}a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{1}}^{\dagger}

\displaystyle\ +\rho_{00}\Pi_{0}(H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}+E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}})\Pi_{0}{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{0}}a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{0}}^{\dagger}\bigr]

\displaystyle=\mathbf{y}_{1}\cdot\mathbf{a}^{1}_{1}+\mathbf{y}_{0}\cdot\mathbf{a}^{1}_{0}

\displaystyle=\mathbf{y}_{1}\cdot{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{1}}\mathbf{a}^{1}+\mathbf{y}_{0}\cdot{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{0}}\mathbf{a}^{1},

(A.88)

where
\mathbf{{a}}^{1}_{1}
and
\mathbf{{a}}^{1}_{0}
are vectors whose components are the eigenvalues of the matrix
a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}
in, as of yet, undetermined permutations (fixed by
{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{1}}
and
{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{0}}
). The vector
\mathbf{a}^{1}
, in turn, collects exactly these eigenvalues in non-increasing order. However, since the energy basis for the pointer has been fixed by Eq. (
A.87
), the vectors
\mathbf{y}_{1}
and
\mathbf{y}_{0}
are completely determined.
Their components are given by

\displaystyle(y_{1})_{i}\,=\,\rho_{11}s_{2i+1},\quad(y_{0})_{i}\,=\,\rho_{00}(s_{2i}+E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}})\,.

(A.89)

To minimise the energy in the non-correlated subspace, we are thus looking for the solution to the optimisation problem

\displaystyle\min E_{\mathrm{nc}}(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})

\displaystyle=\min_{M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{0},M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{1}}(\mathbf{y}_{0}\cdot M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{0}\mathbf{a}^{1}+\mathbf{y}_{1}\cdot M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{1}\mathbf{a}^{1})\,,

(A.90)

i.e., to find the optimal permutation matrices
M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{0}
and
M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{1}
. Because of the freedom to choose these two permutations independently, the optimizations in the two subspaces decouple and it can be easily seen that the optimal solution for both is to pair up the smallest energies with the largest weights. In other words, to select
M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{0}=M^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{1}=\mathds{1}
. For
\rho_{00}=\rho_{11}
, this in turn implies

\displaystyle\min E_{\mathrm{nc}}(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})

\displaystyle=\,(\mathbf{y}_{0}+\mathbf{y}_{1})\cdot\mathbf{a}^{1}\,=\,\mathbf{x}^{*}\mathbf{a}^{1}\,.

(A.91)

where we have noted that for the special case
6
6

6

This equality holds in the special case that
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}=2
, for the more general case see Appendix
A.IX
.
of
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}=2
one may collect
\mathbf{y}_{0}
and
\mathbf{y}_{1}
into
\mathbf{y}_{0}+\mathbf{y}_{1}=\mathbf{x}^{*}
.

M^{*}\mathbf{a}^{1}
must be ordered in non-increasing order to achieve the global minimum, which in turn implies that
M_{*}=\mathds{1}
.

Collecting the results for the correlated and non-correlated subspaces and substituting for the forms of
\mathbf{a}^{0}
and
\mathbf{a}^{1}
, the total energy after the interaction is

\displaystyle\min E(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})

\displaystyle=\min(E_{\mathrm{corr}}(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})+E_{\mathrm{nc}}(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}))

\displaystyle=\,\mathbf{x}^{*}\cdot(\mathbf{{a}^{0}}+\mathbf{{a}^{1}})

(A.92)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{2^{N-1}-1}\bigl(s_{2i}+s_{2i+1}+E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\bigr)(p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}+p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{i}).

Since the initial state is diagonal w.r.t. the energy eigenbasis, the initial energy can also be easily computed to be

\displaystyle E(\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{2^{N-1}-1}\bigl[(2s_{i}+E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}})p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}

(A.93)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ +(2s_{(2^{N-1}+i)}+E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}})p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{i}\bigr].

Thus from the above and Eq. (
A.93
) we have

\displaystyle\Delta E_{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$\mathrm{I\hskip-0.5ptI}$}}}

\displaystyle=E(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})-E(\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})

(A.94)

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{i=0}^{2^{N-1}-1}(s_{2i}+s_{2i+1}-2s_{i})p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}

\displaystyle\qquad\quad+(s_{2i}+s_{2i+1}-2s_{(2^{N-1}+i)})p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}_{i},

where we note that,
E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
(the gap of the system) no longer plays any role. Finally, observe that the cost of correlating is always finite. If one substitutes for the
p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(j)$}}}_{i}
from Eq. (
A.65
) and takes the limit in which the pointer is in a pure state, i.e.,
\beta\rightarrow\infty
, then the maximal correlation indeed is perfect correlation,
C=1
, and the corresponding cost of correlating is given by

\displaystyle\lim_{\beta\rightarrow\infty}\Delta E_{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$\mathrm{I\hskip-0.5ptI}$}}}=\Delta E_{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$\mathrm{I\hskip-0.5ptI}$}}}^{\,(C=1)}=\tfrac{1}{2}E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}\,.

(A.95)

Notably, this expression is independent of
N
and hence true also when
N=1
. Therefore, regardless of how many qubits the pointer consists of, if these qubits are initially in the ground state, the cost of correlating the system and pointer is precisely the cost of exciting only a single qubit (modulated by
\rho_{00}=\rho_{11}=\tfrac{1}{2}
).

These results can equivalently be expressed in terms of the sector notation introduced in Appendix
A.I
. In this case, the projectors in Eq. (
A.87
) become

\displaystyle\Pi_{0}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{1}\sum_{i=0}^{2^{N-2}-1}\left|\right.\!{E_{2i}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{E_{2i}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}}\!\left.\right|,

(A.96a)

\displaystyle\Pi_{1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{1}\sum_{i=0}^{2^{N-2}-1}\left|\right.\!{E_{2i+1}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{E_{2i+1}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}}\!\left.\right|\,.

(A.96b)

Similarly, the energy after optimally correlating in Eq. (
A.VIII
) is

\displaystyle E(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{1}\sum_{i=0}^{2^{N-2}-1}\bigl(E_{2i}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(j)$}}}+E_{2i+1}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(j)$}}}+E_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\bigl)

(A.97)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \times(p_{i+j2^{N-2}}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}+p_{i+j2^{N-2}}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}),

and the cost of correlating is

\displaystyle\Delta E_{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$\mathrm{I\hskip-0.5ptI}$}}}

\displaystyle=\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{j=0}^{1}\!\!\!\sum_{i=0}^{2^{N\!-\!2}\!-\!1}\!\!\!\bigl[\bigl(E_{2i}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(j)$}}}\!+\!E_{2i+1}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(j)$}}}\!-\!2E_{i+j2^{N-2}}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}\bigl)p_{i+j2^{N-2}}^{{}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}}

\displaystyle\qquad\ \ \ +\bigl(E_{2i}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(j)$}}}+E_{2i+1}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(j)$}}}-2E_{i+j2^{N-2}}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}\bigl)p_{i+j2^{N-2}}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(1)$}}}\bigr].

(A.98)

A.IX
Construction of the optimal unitary for arbitrary systems

In the previous appendix we provided the construction for correlating a qubit system with an
N-
qubit pointer to
C_{\text{max}}
. We also proved that this construction was an energy minimum. This construction generalises to any unknown quantum systems
\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}=\tfrac{1}{d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}}\mathds{1}_{d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}}
and thermal pointers
\tau_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}(\beta)
, with arbitrary Hamiltonians
H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
and
H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}
. Below we provide the recipe for constructing such a unitary.

Consider the thermal pointer
\tau_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}(\beta)
and order the spectrum of the pointer Hamiltonian in terms of its excitations into
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
sectors of size
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
, i.e.,
H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}=\sum_{k=0}^{d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}-1}\sum_{i=0}^{d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}-1}E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}_{i}\left|\right.\!{E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}_{i}}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}_{i}}\!\left.\right|
with
E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}_{i}\leq E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k^{\prime})$}}}_{j}\ \forall i,j
for
k^{\prime}>k
.
Diagonalise the pointer and the system in their ordered energy eigenbases,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tau_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}(\beta)&=\sum_{k=0}^{d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}-1}\sum_{i=0}^{d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}-1}p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}_{i}\left|\right.\!{E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}_{i}}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}_{i}}\!\left.\right|,\\
\rho_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}&=\sum_{i}\rho_{ii}\left|\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{i}\!\left.\right|,\end{split}

(A.99)

where
p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}_{i}={1}/{\mathcal{Z}}e^{-\beta E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}_{i}}
.

Assign the largest
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
eigenvalues of the pointer state
\tau_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}(\beta)
(captured in the matrix
a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}
) to the correlated subspace. The form of the correlation matrix is given in Eq. (
A.VII
).
To minimise the energy contribution from the correlated subspace, given by
E_{\mathrm{corr}}(\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}})=\mathrm{tr}[\sum_{i}\tilde{\Pi}_{ii}H_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}]
,
choose the pointer Hilbert space projectors to be

\displaystyle\Pi_{i}

\displaystyle=\sum_{k=0}^{{d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}}-1}\sum_{i=0}^{(d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}^{2})-1}\!\!\!\left|\right.\!{E_{d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\cdot j+i}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{E_{d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\cdot j+i}^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(k)$}}}}\!\left.\right|

(A.100)

\displaystyle\ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \ \forall\,j\in\{0,\cdots,d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}-1\}\,.

This choice fixes the basis vectors for the pointer and thus it remains to distribute the remaining probability weights (the remaining eigenvalues of
\tau_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}
) in the non-correlated subspace. This is achieved by pairing the largest weights with the smallest energies. The remaining weights are

\displaystyle a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(i)$}}}

\displaystyle=\,\bigl(\text{diag}(p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{0},\cdots,p^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{2^{N-1}-1})\bigl)\quad i\in\{1,\cdots,d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}-1\},

(A.101)

and the resulting correlation matrix, arising from the optimal unitary
U_{\text{opt}}
has the form

\displaystyle\Gamma_{U_{\text{opt}}}=\!\!\begin{array}[]{c@{\!\!\!}l}\left[\begin{array}[]{c|c|c|c}\!\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{}{{}}{}{{}{}}{{}}{}

\lxSVG@closescope }}}

\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\raisebox{2.0pt}{\footnotesize{$\rho_{d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}-1\,d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}-1}{a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}}$}}\hbox to0pt{\vbox to0pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 0.0pt\lower 0.0pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin} \immediate;
\lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}\end{array}\right]\end{array}.

In turn, this fixes the the matrices
\tilde{A}_{ij}
in Eq. (
A.VII
) to be

\displaystyle\tilde{A}_{ij}=a^{(\pi[i,j])}\,

(A.106)

where
\pi[m,k]
denotes the
m,k-
th element of the
d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}\times d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}
matrix composed of permutations of the entries of the set
\{0,1,\ldots,d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}-1\}
under the constraint that the diagonal entries
\pi[m,m]=0\ \,,\forall m
. For the optimal energy solution, this permutation matrix takes the form

\displaystyle\pi=\left(\begin{array}[]{ccccc}0&1&1&1&\ldots\\
1&0&2&2&\ldots\\
2&2&0&3&\ldots\\
3&3&3&0&\ldots\\
\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\vdots&\ddots\\
\end{array}\right),

which encodes how the correlations in the matrices
a^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(i)$}}}
are paired with the non-correlated subspaces.
The final state admits a simplified form, namely

\displaystyle\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}=\sum_{k=0}^{d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}-1}\frac{\rho_{kk}}{\mathcal{Z}}\Bigl(\sum_{i=0}^{d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}-1}e^{-\beta E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(0)$}}}_{i}}\left|\right.\!{k}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{k}\!\left.\right|\otimes\left|\right.\!{E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(i)$}}}_{k}}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(i)$}}}_{k}}\!\left.\right|

(A.112)

\displaystyle\ \ +\sum_{m\neq k}^{d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}-1}\sum_{i=0}^{d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$P$}}}/d_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S$}}}-1}e^{-\beta E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(\pi[m,k])$}}}_{i}}\left|\right.\!{m}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{m}\!\left.\right|\otimes\left|\right.\!{E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(i)$}}}_{k}}\!\left.\right\rangle\!\!\left\langle\right.\!{E^{\hskip 0.0pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$(i)$}}}_{k}}\!\left.\right|\Bigr).

It can be seen that this state is not unique due to the inherent description in terms of energy. Thus the final state
\tilde{\rho}_{\hskip-0.5pt\raisebox{0.0pt}{\tiny{$S\hskip-1.0ptP$}}}
depends on one’s choice of how to represent the basis and excitations and in general is degenerate. We leave it as an open investigation as to whether, within this class there is a preferred state with special and interesting properties.
